The j-loop runs in the same\n// order as the original code so the floating-point summation is identical.\ntemplate <int BS>\n__global__ void __launch_bounds__(BS) stepKernel(const double4* __restrict__ posIn, double4* __restrict__ posOut,\n                                                 double4* __restrict__ vel, int n, int iBegin, int iEnd) {\n    __shared__ double4 tile[BS];\n    const int i = iBegin + blockIdx.x * BS + threadIdx.x;\n    const bool active = i < iEnd;\n    const double4 pi = active ? posIn[i] : make_double4(0.0, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0);\n    double Fx = 0.0, Fy = 0.0, Fz = 0.0;\n\n    for (int base = 0; {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CfiSm6EvnjSLZCqNgj4T1","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The nbody simulation now runs entirely on the GPU with CUDA, and for every test size its final positions and velocities are bit-for-bit identical to the original. The changes are only in `nbody/nbody.cpp` and `nbody/CMakeLists.txt`, and the executable is still called `nbody`.\n\n**Results** (10 steps, RTX 3090s; the GPU count is chosen automatically):\n\n| Bodies | Original CPU | CUDA |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1,024 | 77 ms | 6 ms |\n| 16,384 | 19.9 s | 336 ms on 1 GPU / 115 ms on 4 GPUs |\n| 65,536 | — | 4.75 s / 2.44 s / 1.26 s on 1 / 2 / 4 GPUs |\n\nI compared result hashes from `-r` against a build of the original code for N = 1, 2, 1000, 1024, 4096, 10007 and 16384, including runs split across several GPUs: all matched exactly. `-v` passes. N=0 and 0 steps also work.\n\n**What changed:**\n- **One kernel per step.** Each GPU thread handles one body. Body positions are loaded in shared-memory chunks, and the force and position updates happen in the same kernel. The kernel reads positions from one buffer and writes the new ones to a second, then the two swap. This gives the same result as the original's separate force and update loops, without a second kernel launch.\n- **Matching the original's arithmetic exactly.** Each body still adds up forces from the other bodies in the original order. On this machine, the compiler builds the original code with fused multiply-adds (FMA) in specific places. The kernel uses FMA in exactly those places and nowhere else (the CUDA code is compiled with `--fmad=false`). Division and square root stay exactly rounded. That is why the output matches bit for bit, not just approximately.\n- **Multiple GPUs.** Bodies are split evenly across all visible GPUs once there are at least 2048 per GPU; below that, only one GPU is used, which measured faster. After each step the GPUs exchange their updated positions. These GPUs can't copy directly to each other, so the copies go through host memory.\n- **Block size** shrinks for small N so that all the GPU's processors still get work.\n- **Validation energy** (`-v`) is also computed on the GPU. Its printed value can differ from the original in about the 13th significant digit (for example `…709.499866` vs `…709.504816` at N=10007). That's because the sum is added up in a different order, and it doesn't affect the simulation output.\n- **Timing:** GPU start-up now happens before the timer starts, so \"Simulation time\" measures only the simulation, as before.\n- **CMake:** `nbody.cpp` is compiled as CUDA for the local GPU type, with the same host flags as before (`-O3 -march=native -Wall -Wextra`). It links only the CUDA runtime that comes with the toolkit, so there are no new dependencies. A clean build gives no warnings.\n\nThe exact match depends on the original being compiled by the same compiler with the same flags. With a different compiler, the reference itself would round differently in the last bits."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":91,"cache_read_input_tokens":40276,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":91},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"2140f73a-f26a-4caf-a48d-cd8a14b3a9d8","uuid":"4c4437b5-b806-47a5-bcd6-a7810dc5a5be","timestamp":"2026-10-05T04:17:47.215Z","request_id":"req_011CfiSm5pPVYbttEhKrp417"}
